#### LDF in turmoil, strong dissent against CPI(M) leadership: Keralam CM Satheesan

**In short:** Keralam Chief Minister V D Satheesan said on Wednesday that the ruling LDF was in “turmoil,” with CPI(M) and CPI locked in a bitter war of words and strong dissent brewing against CPI(M) leadership, while the Congress was “watching it from outside and enjoying it.” Responding to the Left allies’ row over the Deputy Leader of Opposition post, he accused the CPI(M) of mobilising its student and youth wings for violent street protests to divert attention from party issues, apparently referring to Tuesday’s SFI march over the complete rendition of Vande Mataram at an event attended by Vice President C P Radhakrishnan, during which several activists were injured in a clash with police.

#### Withdrawal of cases standard admin practice: DMK on Vijay

**In short:** Senior DMK leader T K S Elangovan on Wednesday downplayed the political significance of Tamil Nadu Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay’s decision to withdraw police cases against protesting farmers and teachers, calling it standard administrative practice and noting previous DMK governments had done the same when people fought for their rights. Vijay told the Assembly that cases against farmers who peacefully protested Parandur airport and SIPCOT expansion, and against teachers who protested democratically, would be dropped; Elangovan also dismissed speculation about TMC MP Saayoni Ghosh joining Vijay’s party and rejected suggestions Vijay could be a 2029 prime ministerial face, saying his TVK government lacks an absolute majority in Tamil Nadu.

#### SIR is helping BJP, SC must stop deletions: Sibal

**In short:** Rajya Sabha MP Kapil Sibal on Wednesday alleged that the Election Commission’s Special Intensive Revision (SIR) of electoral rolls is aimed at helping the BJP win elections and urged the Supreme Court to stop “bulk deletions,” citing an Indian Express report that BJP agents in Godda, Jharkhand, sought to delete voters mostly from the minority community. At a press conference, Sibal said BJP workers were filing bulk Form 7 deletion requests without giving reasons and that targeted names belonged to the Muslim community, with affected residents saying their families had lived there for years.

#### Keralam lays groundwork for India’s first electric truck corridor on NH 66

**In short:** Keralam is preparing to develop the 622-km National Highway 66 stretch from Kasaragod to Thiruvananthapuram as India’s first electric truck corridor, with a KSEBL–ICCT study finding an initial 12.5 MW charging network requiring Rs 19 crore investment and projecting 1,939 chargers with 204.5 MW combined capacity and Rs 338 crore investment by 2050. Researchers used FASTag, GPS and parking data from 13,355 trucks along the corridor; Kochi, Kozhikode and Kollam are expected to emerge as major charging hubs, and KSEB said the project can draw from central PM E-DRIVE scheme funds and Rs 63.12 crore already sanctioned for Keralam’s charging network.

#### JioHotstar launches services in UK, Canada, Singapore

**In short:** JioHotstar, the OTT platform owned by JioStar, on Wednesday announced its international launch in the UK, Canada and Singapore, marking its foray into becoming a global entertainment platform with over 1,60,000 hours of content across 12-plus languages. The streaming platform, with over 500 million active users, said the launch will offer personalised recommendations, interactive features and flexible subscription options alongside its content catalogue, and will target the South Asian diaspora while also appealing to a broader global audience.

JioHotstar, the OTT platform owned by JioStar, on Wednesday announced its international launch in the UK, Canada and Singapore, marking its foray into becoming a global entertainment platform with over 1,60,000 hours of content across 12-plus languages. The streaming platform, with over 500 million active users, said the launch will offer personalised recommendations, interactive features and flexible subscription options alongside its content catalogue, and will target the South Asian diaspora while also appealing to a broader global audience. It will add over 30,000 hours of fresh content annually across these markets, including new originals, iconic franchises and vast television content.

#### Gen Z missing career opportunities due to fear of being judged: Report

**In short:** Fear of being judged holds back the majority of Gen Z professionals from connecting or networking with people who could help them in their careers, a LinkedIn report said on Wednesday, with 83 per cent of India's Gen Z professionals hesitating to interact or network with such people. Fear of being judged (44 per cent), concerns about bothering others (39 per cent) and worries about sounding inauthentic (37 per cent) are preventing Gen Z from seeking valuable advice and making connections, according to a Censuswide survey of 18,050 respondents conducted between July 20 and August 11, 2026 across countries including India.

Fear of being judged holds back the majority of Gen Z professionals from connecting or networking with people who could help them in their careers, a LinkedIn report said on Wednesday, with 83 per cent of India's Gen Z professionals hesitating to interact or network with such people. Fear of being judged (44 per cent), concerns about bothering others (39 per cent) and worries about sounding inauthentic (37 per cent) are preventing Gen Z from seeking valuable advice and making connections, according to a Censuswide survey of 18,050 respondents conducted between July 20 and August 11, 2026 across countries including India. Indian job applicants with an inside connection were nearly 8.5 times more likely to get hired than those without one, yet 59 per cent of Gen Z said no one has clearly shown them how to build professional relationships.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Gen Z missing career opportunities due to fear of being judged: Report
- **Told:** Fear of being judged holds back the majority of Gen Z professionals from connecting or networking with people who could help them in their careers, a LinkedIn report said on Wednesday, with 83 per cent of India's Gen Z professionals hesitating to interact or network with such people.
- **Purpose:** Indian job applicants with an inside connection were nearly 8.5 times more likely to get hired than those without one, yet 59 per cent of Gen Z said no one has clearly shown them how to build professional relationships.

**Important points**

#### Strictly follow food safety norms: FSSAI tells 5-star hotels

**In short:** Food Safety Standards Authority of India (FSSAI) CEO Rajit Punhani, in a meeting with senior executives of major hotel companies held on Tuesday, asked all leading 5-star hospitality firms in the country to strictly follow food safety norms and ensure hygiene benchmarks, asserting zero tolerance for any violations. The direction comes in the wake of FSSAI's recent notices to two major 5-star hotels, JW Marriott and Andaz at Aerocity in Delhi, and amid an enhanced drive and stringent actions against food safety violations.

Food Safety Standards Authority of India (FSSAI) CEO Rajit Punhani, in a meeting with senior executives of major hotel companies held on Tuesday, asked all leading 5-star hospitality firms in the country to strictly follow food safety norms and ensure hygiene benchmarks, asserting zero tolerance for any violations. The direction comes in the wake of FSSAI's recent notices to two major 5-star hotels, JW Marriott and Andaz at Aerocity in Delhi, and amid an enhanced drive and stringent actions against food safety violations. The regulator told 5-star hotels it has ramped up inspections across such hotels, while major hotel operators claimed they are adhering to safety norms and presented positive progress updates on their compliance frameworks.

#### Modi govt defends new GDP estimates

**In short:** The statistics ministry on Wednesday defended the methodology behind its newly released economic growth estimates, saying revisions to last year's GDP and the divergence between different price measures reflect updated data and estimation techniques rather than an attempt to artificially boost headline growth. India's economy grew 7.8 per cent in real terms in the first quarter of fiscal 2026-27 according to the revised GDP series, which uses 2022-23 as the base year and incorporates a new Producer Price Index, Banking Services Price Index and additional administrative data.

The statistics ministry on Wednesday defended the methodology behind its newly released economic growth estimates, saying revisions to last year's GDP and the divergence between different price measures reflect updated data and estimation techniques rather than an attempt to artificially boost headline growth. India's economy grew 7.8 per cent in real terms in the first quarter of fiscal 2026-27 according to the revised GDP series, which uses 2022-23 as the base year and incorporates a new Producer Price Index, Banking Services Price Index and additional administrative data. The ministry rejected claims that the Q1 2025-26 current-price GDP estimate was reduced from Rs 86.05 lakh crore to Rs 80 lakh crore to make the latest growth rate appear stronger, noting the Rs 86.05 lakh crore figure was under the superseded 2011-12 base-year series and was revised to Rs 80.32 lakh crore when the 2022-23 base-year series was introduced in February 2026.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Modi govt defends new GDP estimates
- **Told:** The statistics ministry on Wednesday defended the methodology behind its newly released economic growth estimates, saying revisions to last year's GDP and the divergence between different price measures reflect updated data and estimation techniques rather than an attempt to artificially boost headline growth.
- **Purpose:** The ministry rejected claims that the Q1 2025-26 current-price GDP estimate was reduced from Rs 86.05 lakh crore to Rs 80 lakh crore to make the latest growth rate appear stronger, noting the Rs 86.05 lakh crore figure was under the superseded 2011-12 base-year series and was revised to Rs 80.32 lakh crore when the 2022-23 base-year series was introduced in February 2026.

**Important points**
- The ministry rejected claims that the Q1 2025-26 current-price GDP estimate was reduced from Rs 86.05 lakh crore to Rs 80 lakh crore to make the latest growth rate appear stronger, noting the Rs 86.05 lakh crore figure was under the superseded 2011-12 base-year series and was revised to Rs 80.32 lakh crore when the 2022-23 base-year series was introduced in February 2026.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.11_

#### 'Share of electricity in India's energy mix to reach 45-50%'

**In short:** India will double the share of electricity in its total energy mix to 45-50 per cent by 2047 from 22 per cent at present, Central Electricity Authority Chairperson Ghanshyam Prasad said on Wednesday at Bharat Electricity 2026, POWERGEN India & Indian Utility Week 2026. Last year India added a record 64,000 MW of new capacity, with more than 50,000 MW from renewables alone, and this year is likely to surpass that with up to 70,000 MW of fresh additions; by 2035 India is poised to double its 2030 renewables target, reach 100 GW of pumped hydro storage by 2035-36 and achieve 100 GW of nuclear power by 2047.

India will double the share of electricity in its total energy mix to 45-50 per cent by 2047 from 22 per cent at present, Central Electricity Authority Chairperson Ghanshyam Prasad said on Wednesday at Bharat Electricity 2026, POWERGEN India & Indian Utility Week 2026. Last year India added a record 64,000 MW of new capacity, with more than 50,000 MW from renewables alone, and this year is likely to surpass that with up to 70,000 MW of fresh additions; by 2035 India is poised to double its 2030 renewables target, reach 100 GW of pumped hydro storage by 2035-36 and achieve 100 GW of nuclear power by 2047. Prasad said Rs 4 lakh crore (USD 40 billion) in transmission projects are underway to evacuate 266 GW, with 600 GVA of transformation capacity planned to provide grid connectivity for new renewables within 24-36 months.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 'Share of electricity in India's energy mix to reach 45-50%'
- **Told:** India will double the share of electricity in its total energy mix to 45-50 per cent by 2047 from 22 per cent at present, Central Electricity Authority Chairperson Ghanshyam Prasad said on Wednesday at Bharat Electricity 2026, POWERGEN India & Indian Utility Week 2026.
- **Purpose:** Prasad said Rs 4 lakh crore (USD 40 billion) in transmission projects are underway to evacuate 266 GW, with 600 GVA of transformation capacity planned to provide grid connectivity for new renewables within 24-36 months.

**Important points**
- Prasad said Rs 4 lakh crore (USD 40 billion) in transmission projects are underway to evacuate 266 GW, with 600 GVA of transformation capacity planned to provide grid connectivity for new renewables within 24-36 months.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.11_

#### India's e-commerce market to hit $ 345 bn by 2030; dark stores to triple, says a report

**In short:** India's e-commerce sector is projected to grow nearly three times to USD 345 billion by 2030 from USD 125 billion in 2024 at a compound annual growth rate of 18.4 per cent through 2030, according to a report titled 'Smart Growth in a Fast Market' by research consultancy Infisum released on Wednesday. The country's dark store network is set to nearly triple from 2,525 in 2025 to approximately 7,500 by 2030, with quick commerce valued at an estimated USD 65-70 billion by 2030 and expected to contribute 45-50 per cent of incremental e-retail growth; Blinkit leads with a 44 per cent market share, followed by Zepto and Swiggy Instamart at 25 per cent and 20 per cent respectively.

India's e-commerce sector is projected to grow nearly three times to USD 345 billion by 2030 from USD 125 billion in 2024 at a compound annual growth rate of 18.4 per cent through 2030, according to a report titled 'Smart Growth in a Fast Market' by research consultancy Infisum released on Wednesday. The country's dark store network is set to nearly triple from 2,525 in 2025 to approximately 7,500 by 2030, with quick commerce valued at an estimated USD 65-70 billion by 2030 and expected to contribute 45-50 per cent of incremental e-retail growth; Blinkit leads with a 44 per cent market share, followed by Zepto and Swiggy Instamart at 25 per cent and 20 per cent respectively. By the end of the decade e-commerce is expected to account for 10-12 per cent of India's total retail spending and contribute 2.5 per cent to the national GDP, serving an estimated 420-440 million online shoppers.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** India's e-commerce market to hit $ 345 bn by 2030; dark stores to triple, says a report
- **Told:** India's e-commerce sector is projected to grow nearly three times to USD 345 billion by 2030 from USD 125 billion in 2024 at a compound annual growth rate of 18.4 per cent through 2030, according to a report titled 'Smart Growth in a Fast Market' by research consultancy Infisum released on Wednesday.
- **Purpose:** By the end of the decade e-commerce is expected to account for 10-12 per cent of India's total retail spending and contribute 2.5 per cent to the national GDP, serving an estimated 420-440 million online shoppers.

**Important points**
- By the end of the decade e-commerce is expected to account for 10-12 per cent of India's total retail spending and contribute 2.5 per cent to the national GDP, serving an estimated 420-440 million online shoppers.

#### Flexi-cap funds attract nearly Rs 50,000 cr

**In short:** Flexi-cap mutual fund schemes attracted nearly Rs 50,000 crore in net inflows during January-July 2026, marking a 27 per cent increase over the year-ago period, as investors increasingly favoured equity strategies that can dynamically move across market-cap segments. Flexi-cap fund folios rose to 2.44 crore in July 2026 from 1.94 crore a year earlier, adding around 50 lakh investor accounts, while assets under management of the category stood at around Rs 6 lakh crore as of July 2026, up from Rs 4.94 lakh crore in July 2025, according to data from the Association of Mutual Funds in India (Amfi).

Flexi-cap mutual fund schemes attracted nearly Rs 50,000 crore in net inflows during January-July 2026, marking a 27 per cent increase over the year-ago period, as investors increasingly favoured equity strategies that can dynamically move across market-cap segments. Flexi-cap funds received Rs 4,709 crore in July, compared with Rs 5,231 crore in June.

#### L&T Finance plans to add 500 gold loan branches

**In short:** L&T Finance plans to add at least 500 dedicated gold loan branches annually over the next four to five years, taking its network to 2,500-3,000 branches, Whole-time Director and Chief Operating Officer Raju Dodti said on Wednesday. The company, which currently has 343 active gold loan branches, expects to reach 800 branches by the end of the current financial year; its gold finance business, acquired from a Chandigarh-based company in June 2025 with 130 branches, had established an additional 200-plus branches by March 2026.

#### In insolvency case, Chandra opposes NCLT

**In short:** Essel Group Chairman Subhash Chandra on Wednesday opposed the formation of a five-member bench by the NCLT to decide his personal insolvency case, contending that the tribunal does not have the power to constitute such a bench, with Senior Advocate Sasmit Patra appearing for him before the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal (NCLAT) terming the NCLT order faulty and wrong. The five-member bench had on Tuesday stayed the order of Nilesh Sharma, Member (Judicial), who was brought in as the third member after a division bench of the NCLT delivered a split verdict; NCLT on Tuesday barred Chandra from alienating his assets and stayed an order allowing him to settle claims arising from personal guarantees on group borrowings for about Rs 6.5 crore against claims of roughly Rs 22,006 crore.

#### Formulate rules for fishing vessel transit to EEZ, SC directs T.N.

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Wednesday directed Tamil Nadu to frame rules creating a "specified channel" or official navigation corridor under the Marine Fishing Regulation Rules 2020 for transit of vessels from territorial waters to the EEZ for purse seine fishing. A Division Bench of Justices P.S.

The Supreme Court on Wednesday directed Tamil Nadu to frame rules creating a "specified channel" or official navigation corridor under the Marine Fishing Regulation Rules 2020 for transit of vessels from territorial waters to the EEZ for purse seine fishing. A Division Bench of Justices P.S. Narasimha and Alok Aradhe upheld both the state's Marine Fishing Regulation Rules 2020 and the Centre's Sustainable Harnessing of Fisheries in the EEZ Rules 2025, noting the EEZ Rules contemplate an "access pass," after the state raised concern that fishers could employ purse seine nets within territorial waters under the guise of EEZ fishing. The court held that rights and liabilities are governed by the relevant laws and said there is no conflict between the Union's EEZ Rules and Tamil Nadu's marine fishery regulation rules.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Formulate rules for fishing vessel transit to EEZ, SC directs T.N
- **Told:** The Supreme Court on Wednesday directed Tamil Nadu to frame rules creating a "specified channel" or official navigation corridor under the Marine Fishing Regulation Rules 2020 for transit of vessels from territorial waters to the EEZ for purse seine fishing.
- **Purpose:** The court held that rights and liabilities are governed by the relevant laws and said there is no conflict between the Union's EEZ Rules and Tamil Nadu's marine fishery regulation rules.

**Important points**
- Narasimha and Alok Aradhe upheld both the state's Marine Fishing Regulation Rules 2020 and the Centre's Sustainable Harnessing of Fisheries in the EEZ Rules 2025, noting the EEZ Rules contemplate an "access pass," after the state raised concern that fishers could employ purse seine nets within territorial waters under the guise of EEZ fishing.
- The court held that rights and liabilities are governed by the relevant laws and said there is no conflict between the Union's EEZ Rules and Tamil Nadu's marine fishery regulation rules.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.5_

#### Agniveer involvement ruled out in military weapon theft

**In short:** Malkajgiri Police have reportedly ruled out Agniveers in the theft of service weapons from the premises of 20 Madras Regiment in Bollarum, Secunderabad Cantonment, with Military Intelligence joining the investigation at an undisclosed location inside the defence area. The theft included four AK-203 assault rifles, an INSAS 1B1 rifle, six 9 mm Auto 1A pistols, a civilian 6.35-bore pistol, a passive night-vision binocular, and more than 1,800 rounds of ammunition.

The theft included four AK-203 assault rifles, an INSAS 1B1 rifle, six 9 mm Auto 1A pistols, a civilian 6.35-bore pistol, a passive night-vision binocular, and more than 1,800 rounds of ammunition. It was detected around 7.50 a.m. on Monday when the Kote and Battalion Magazine were opened and locks were found broken; the weapons were last accounted for around 6 p.m. on August 30.

#### Sunni leader's observations on women unsuitable for modern Keralam, says CM

**In short:** Chief Minister V.D. Satheesan rejected Sunni leader Kanthapuram A.P.

Chief Minister V.D. Satheesan rejected Sunni leader Kanthapuram A.P. Aboobacker Musliar's controversial opinions against women's participation in public platforms, saying the comments are unsuitable for modern progressive Keralam and that neither the Congress nor the State government can agree with a stand belonging to the 19th century. After a Cabinet meeting press conference, Satheesan cited Islamic history including Khadija as a businesswoman and Ayesha leading an army. The Kerala Muslim Jamaat condemned his remarks that Musliar had misinterpreted Islam and demanded their withdrawal and an apology.

#### CBI hosts India-Thailand meet on 'Live Animal Trafficking'

**In short:** The Central Bureau of Investigation has organised a two-day case coordination meeting between India and Thailand on 'Live Animal Trafficking', which commenced on Wednesday, co-hosted with the Interpol Environmental Security Sub-Directorate. The agency said smuggling of exotic live animals and birds for the pet trade is a growing trend in Asia, with animals trafficked mainly by air from Thailand, Malaysia, and Indonesia to South Asian countries including India, using Singapore as a transit point.

#### SC seeks govt. explanation on IPS deputation in CAPFs

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Wednesday sought an explanation from Union Home Secretary Govind Mohan on implementing its May 23, 2025 judgment to progressively reduce IPS deputation to senior CAPF posts within two years, as Justice Ujjal Bhuyan said cadre officers were "completely stifled". An affidavit said 46 IPS officers were brought on deputation to CAPFs up to the SAG level after the judgment, while the government enacted the CAPFs (General Administration) Act, 2026, published on April 9, now challenged in five writ petitions listed for November 18.

#### Bhupathi: from dreaded Maoist to Gondwana University coordinator

**In short:** Bhupathi, once a Maoist with a ₹6 crore bounty wanted in six States, surrendered with 60 others on October 15, 2025, before Maharashtra Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis, and has now been selected coordinator at Gondwana University's Village Council Capacity Building and Training Centre. Coordinators will visit Gadchiroli villages to supervise Gram Sabhas and create awareness of tribal rights under PESA, receiving a ₹30,000 monthly stipend and travel allowance.

- Bhupathi, once a Maoist with a ₹6 crore bounty wanted in six States, surrendered with 60 others on October 15, 2025, before Maharashtra Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis, and has now been selected coordinator at Gondwana University's Village Council Capacity Building and Training Centre. Coordinators will visit Gadchiroli villages to supervise Gram Sabhas and create awareness of tribal rights under PESA, receiving a ₹30,000 monthly stipend and travel allowance.

#### What's the reason behind the sudden surge in onion prices?

**In short:** On September 1, the all-India average onion price was ₹49.59 a kg, up over 74% from ₹28.48 on the corresponding day in 2025, though production is estimated at 307.37 lakh tonnes in 2025-26, in line with last year's 307.67 lakh tonnes. Industry sources cite lower-quality harvests after delayed rains in Maharashtra, hoarding by big traders, and seasonal festive demand; the government has begun calibrated buffer releases, selling onions at ₹35 a kg.

#### Can jurists be appointed as Supreme Court judges?

**In short:** Supreme Court judge Justice Ujjal Bhuyan questioned at NLU Delhi's 13th LL.M. convocation why Article 124(3)'s route for appointing a 'distinguished jurist' as a Supreme Court judge has remained unused for more than 76 years, noting no legal academic has been elevated under the provision while judges have overwhelmingly come from High Court benches and only 11 advocates were directly elevated from the Bar.

Supreme Court judge Justice Ujjal Bhuyan questioned at NLU Delhi's 13th LL.M. convocation why Article 124(3)'s route for appointing a 'distinguished jurist' as a Supreme Court judge has remained unused for more than 76 years, noting no legal academic has been elevated under the provision while judges have overwhelmingly come from High Court benches and only 11 advocates were directly elevated from the Bar. The explainer traces Constituent Assembly intent to diversify the Bench, the rejected and later omitted High Court jurist provision, and barriers including Collegium recommendation and restrictions on full-time law teachers practising as advocates.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Can jurists be appointed as Supreme Court judges?
- **Told:** Supreme Court judge Justice Ujjal Bhuyan questioned at NLU Delhi's 13th LL.M.
- **Purpose:** The explainer traces Constituent Assembly intent to diversify the Bench, the rejected and later omitted High Court jurist provision, and barriers including Collegium recommendation and restrictions on full-time law teachers practising as advocates.

**Important points**
- The explainer traces Constituent Assembly intent to diversify the Bench, the rejected and later omitted High Court jurist provision, and barriers including Collegium recommendation and restrictions on full-time law teachers practising as advocates.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.17_

#### Iran attacks US sites after American strikes, wedding deaths

**In short:** Iran's Revolutionary Guards launched attacks on US bases in Jordan, the UAE, Kuwait, Bahrain and Iraq's autonomous Kurdistan region on Wednesday, as violence surged after extensive American bombardment and the deaths of four people, including a child, at an Iranian wedding in Sirik. US President Donald Trump had warned of "much harder and higher" attacks if Iran retaliated to US strikes that Iran's military and state media said killed 18 people.

#### Nepal flash flood death toll rises to 1,127

**In short:** The death toll from Nepal's devastating flash floods rose to 1,127 on Wednesday as rescuers raced to find thousands still missing a week after the Himalayan disaster, police said. Nepal Police recovered 348 bodies from Chitwan, 217 from Nawalparasi East, 190 from Nawalparasi West, 155 from Nuwakot and others from Gorkha, Dhading, Rasuwa and Tanahu; postmortem and legal identification procedures had been completed for 1,113 bodies and 95 had been handed over to relatives.

The death toll from Nepal's devastating flash floods rose to 1,127 on Wednesday as rescuers raced to find thousands still missing a week after the Himalayan disaster, police said. Nepal Police recovered 348 bodies from Chitwan, 217 from Nawalparasi East, 190 from Nawalparasi West, 155 from Nuwakot and others from Gorkha, Dhading, Rasuwa and Tanahu; postmortem and legal identification procedures had been completed for 1,113 bodies and 95 had been handed over to relatives. Security forces have rescued 6,541 people from affected areas while search operations continued, and details of 911 unidentified bodies were uploaded to the Nepal Police website to help families identify relatives.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Nepal flash flood death toll rises to 1,127
- **Told:** The death toll from Nepal's devastating flash floods rose to 1,127 on Wednesday as rescuers raced to find thousands still missing a week after the Himalayan disaster, police said.
- **Purpose:** Security forces have rescued 6,541 people from affected areas while search operations continued, and details of 911 unidentified bodies were uploaded to the Nepal Police website to help families identify relatives.

**Important points**
- Security forces have rescued 6,541 people from affected areas while search operations continued, and details of 911 unidentified bodies were uploaded to the Nepal Police website to help families identify relatives.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.10; News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

#### Blast near German train station

**In short:** An explosion near Augsburg train station in southern Germany at around 3:15 am on Wednesday injured a 17-year-old girl and an 18-year-old man, both Ukrainian citizens, after an unidentified object exploded in a passageway, prompting police to close the transport hub.

#### US-Iran strikes deepen Middle East crisis

**In short:** The United States military struck targets in Iran on Tuesday as hostilities flared after a month without military action, and Iran fired missiles and drones in response, according to Iranian semiofficial media Fars and Tasnim. One US strike hit a home hosting a wedding, killing five people and wounding dozens, local officials said, while US Central Command said the strikes followed Tehran's attempts to attack commercial shipping in the Strait of Hormuz and US service members in the region.

The United States military struck targets in Iran on Tuesday as hostilities flared after a month without military action, and Iran fired missiles and drones in response, according to Iranian semiofficial media Fars and Tasnim. One US strike hit a home hosting a wedding, killing five people and wounding dozens, local officials said, while US Central Command said the strikes followed Tehran's attempts to attack commercial shipping in the Strait of Hormuz and US service members in the region. President Donald Trump warned further Iranian retaliation would draw harder attacks, the State Department renewed a regionwide Middle East travel alert, Iran is working with Oman on a shipping-traffic framework, the IAEA said it cannot inspect war-hit nuclear sites or report on Iran's enriched uranium stockpile, and Israel detained a senior Hamas security official in a Gaza City raid that killed at least four people including two children.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** US-Iran strikes deepen Middle East crisis
- **Told:** The United States military struck targets in Iran on Tuesday as hostilities flared after a month without military action, and Iran fired missiles and drones in response, according to Iranian semiofficial media Fars and Tasnim.
- **Purpose:** President Donald Trump warned further Iranian retaliation would draw harder attacks, the State Department renewed a regionwide Middle East travel alert, Iran is working with Oman on a shipping-traffic framework, the IAEA said it cannot inspect war-hit nuclear sites or report on Iran's enriched uranium stockpile, and Israel detained a senior Hamas security official in a Gaza City raid that killed at least four people including two children.

**Important points**
- President Donald Trump warned further Iranian retaliation would draw harder attacks, the State Department renewed a regionwide Middle East travel alert.
- Iran is working with Oman on a shipping-traffic framework, the IAEA said it cannot inspect war-hit nuclear sites or report on Iran's enriched uranium stockpile, and Israel detained a senior Hamas security official in a Gaza City raid that killed at least four people including two children.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

#### FBI seizes $5,60,000 in Hamas crypto funds

**In short:** The FBI has seized more than USD 560,000 in cryptocurrency donations intended for Hamas, the Justice Department said Tuesday. In addition to seizing cryptocurrency meant to support Hamas' military wing, the department took control of website domains and communication platforms used for fundraising and recruitment, and obtained information about thousands of people who had contacted Hamas with a goal of giving money to the group.

The FBI has seized more than USD 560,000 in cryptocurrency donations intended for Hamas, the Justice Department said Tuesday. In addition to seizing cryptocurrency meant to support Hamas' military wing, the department took control of website domains and communication platforms used for fundraising and recruitment, and obtained information about thousands of people who had contacted Hamas with a goal of giving money to the group. US attorney Jeanine Pirro said Hamas networks are not secure, its crypto is vulnerable, and the operation would continue until Hamas' ability to wage war is defeated.

**What this is**

#### When roads end, drones carry dead in Nepal

**In short:** In flood-ravaged hills of Nepal, drones airlifted seven bodies this week from Devighat-3 in Nuwakot to Devighat-5 from an area so difficult and dangerous that rescuers could not safely reach it. The operation involved personnel from Nepal Police, Nepal Army and drone manufacturer GarudX, and reflects how drones are quietly becoming an extra pair of hands in Nepal's disaster response—ferrying food and medicines to cut-off communities, searching inaccessible areas and airlifting bodies from locations too dangerous for rescuers.

In flood-ravaged hills of Nepal, drones airlifted seven bodies this week from Devighat-3 in Nuwakot to Devighat-5 from an area so difficult and dangerous that rescuers could not safely reach it. The operation involved personnel from Nepal Police, Nepal Army and drone manufacturer GarudX, and reflects how drones are quietly becoming an extra pair of hands in Nepal's disaster response—ferrying food and medicines to cut-off communities, searching inaccessible areas and airlifting bodies from locations too dangerous for rescuers. In Devighat-3, an AI-enabled drone recently flew over stranded villagers, its loudspeaker asking them a series of simple questions.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** When roads end, drones carry dead in Nepal
- **Told:** In flood-ravaged hills of Nepal, drones airlifted seven bodies this week from Devighat-3 in Nuwakot to Devighat-5 from an area so difficult and dangerous that rescuers could not safely reach it.
- **Purpose:** In Devighat-3, an AI-enabled drone recently flew over stranded villagers, its loudspeaker asking them a series of simple questions.

#### Xi visits Egypt as China seeks Mideast influence

**In short:** Chinese President Xi Jinping's visit to Egypt this week strengthens economic and diplomatic ties as China, the United States' top global rival, cultivates an extensive relationship with one of America's key Middle East allies amid efforts to expand its influence in the rapidly changing region. Xi aims to present China as a stabilizer in the region and contrast it with the United States, whose six-month war with Iran is the latest example of decades of military entanglement there.

Chinese President Xi Jinping's visit to Egypt this week strengthens economic and diplomatic ties as China, the United States' top global rival, cultivates an extensive relationship with one of America's key Middle East allies amid efforts to expand its influence in the rapidly changing region. Xi aims to present China as a stabilizer in the region and contrast it with the United States, whose six-month war with Iran is the latest example of decades of military entanglement there. Xi arrived Tuesday evening at Cairo airport for three days, his first visit there in a decade and first trip to the region since Saudi Arabia in 2022, with the leaders marking 70 years of diplomatic ties through friendly opinion pieces in Egypt's state-run newspapers.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Xi visits Egypt as China seeks Mideast influence
- **Told:** Chinese President Xi Jinping's visit to Egypt this week strengthens economic and diplomatic ties as China, the United States' top global rival, cultivates an extensive relationship with one of America's key Middle East allies amid efforts to expand its influence in the rapidly changing region.
- **Purpose:** Xi arrived Tuesday evening at Cairo airport for three days, his first visit there in a decade and first trip to the region since Saudi Arabia in 2022, with the leaders marking 70 years of diplomatic ties through friendly opinion pieces in Egypt's state-run newspapers.

**Important points**
- Xi arrived Tuesday evening at Cairo airport for three days, his first visit there in a decade and first trip to the region since Saudi Arabia in 2022, with the leaders marking 70 years of diplomatic ties through friendly opinion pieces in Egypt's state-run newspapers.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

#### Levels in 3 Nepal rivers cross danger mark

**In short:** Nepalese authorities on Wednesday urged people living in the central, western and eastern parts of the Himalayan nation to remain alert after water levels in three major rivers crossed alert levels following rainfall in different parts of the country. According to the Department of Hydrology and Meteorology's Flood Forecasting Division, the Sun Koshi River in Sindhuli district, the Budhi Gandaki River in Gorkha and the Mahakali River were above alert levels, and the Marsyangdi River in Lamjung crossed the danger mark at the Bhukundebesi water level monitoring centre at 11:15 PM Tuesday with levels still on the rise.

Nepalese authorities on Wednesday urged people living in the central, western and eastern parts of the Himalayan nation to remain alert after water levels in three major rivers crossed alert levels following rainfall in different parts of the country. According to the Department of Hydrology and Meteorology's Flood Forecasting Division, the Sun Koshi River in Sindhuli district, the Budhi Gandaki River in Gorkha and the Mahakali River were above alert levels, and the Marsyangdi River in Lamjung crossed the danger mark at the Bhukundebesi water level monitoring centre at 11:15 PM Tuesday with levels still on the rise. With increased water flow, the National Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Authority urged people in downstream areas to remain alert as the risk of flooding is high.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Levels in 3 Nepal rivers cross danger mark
- **Told:** Nepalese authorities on Wednesday urged people living in the central, western and eastern parts of the Himalayan nation to remain alert after water levels in three major rivers crossed alert levels following rainfall in different parts of the country.
- **Purpose:** With increased water flow, the National Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Authority urged people in downstream areas to remain alert as the risk of flooding is high.

**Important points**
- With increased water flow, the National Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Authority urged people in downstream areas to remain alert as the risk of flooding is high.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

#### Russia pounds Kyiv for 12 hours, killing 12

**In short:** Russia bombarded the Ukrainian capital for more than 12 hours overnight and into Tuesday, killing at least a dozen people in another aerial onslaught that authorities said was aimed at menacing civilians. The strikes marked an unprecedented sixth straight day of heavy Russian attacks on Kyiv since Russia launched the full-scale invasion of its neighbour more than 4½ years ago, with consecutive aerial assaults designed to grind down civilians by disrupting daily life, Ukrainian officials said.

Russia bombarded the Ukrainian capital for more than 12 hours overnight and into Tuesday, killing at least a dozen people in another aerial onslaught that authorities said was aimed at menacing civilians. The strikes marked an unprecedented sixth straight day of heavy Russian attacks on Kyiv since Russia launched the full-scale invasion of its neighbour more than 4½ years ago, with consecutive aerial assaults designed to grind down civilians by disrupting daily life, Ukrainian officials said. Cruise and ballistic missiles, jet-powered drones, glide bombs and artillery salvos targeted Kyiv as well as eight other regions.

- **Concept:** Russia pounds Kyiv for 12 hours, killing 12

#### Earth is heating past the safe limit; now, the UN wants a way back

**In short:** In a strategic retreat in the fight against climate change, the United Nations has acknowledged that Earth has lost its chance to prevent some of global warming's worst harms and must now figure out how to get temperatures below the danger zone and back to safety. Earth is soon going to exceed the safe temperature threshold agreed upon in the 2015 Paris climate accord because of warming from the burning of coal, oil and gas, according to a bleak first-of-its-kind report issued Wednesday by the United Nations Environment Programme.

In a strategic retreat in the fight against climate change, the United Nations has acknowledged that Earth has lost its chance to prevent some of global warming's worst harms and must now figure out how to get temperatures below the danger zone and back to safety. Earth is soon going to exceed the safe temperature threshold agreed upon in the 2015 Paris climate accord because of warming from the burning of coal, oil and gas, according to a bleak first-of-its-kind report issued Wednesday by the United Nations Environment Programme. UN Secretary-General Antonio Guterres said humanity is speeding past the limit to avoid climate catastrophe, and the new strategy envisions getting back below the threshold by ending fossil fuel use and developing ways to remove industries' carbon dioxide from the air.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Earth is heating past the safe limit; now, the UN wants a way back
- **Told:** In a strategic retreat in the fight against climate change, the United Nations has acknowledged that Earth has lost its chance to prevent some of global warming's worst harms and must now figure out how to get temperatures below the danger zone and back to safety.
- **Purpose:** UN Secretary-General Antonio Guterres said humanity is speeding past the limit to avoid climate catastrophe, and the new strategy envisions getting back below the threshold by ending fossil fuel use and developing ways to remove industries' carbon dioxide from the air.

**Important points**
- UN Secretary-General Antonio Guterres said humanity is speeding past the limit to avoid climate catastrophe, and the new strategy envisions getting back below the threshold by ending fossil fuel use and developing ways to remove industries' carbon dioxide from the air.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.9_

#### UN charts course to manage global warming-limit breach

**In short:** Warning that a breach of the 1.5 degrees Celsius global warming limit will bring irreversible ecological losses, the United Nations Environment Programme on Wednesday set out a detailed "overshoot, peak, and decline" pathway for the first time. The Limiting Overshoot report said global warming would cross 1.5 degrees Celsius in the next few years; even if every country delivers on its national climate plan and net-zero target, peak warming would reach 1.8 degrees Celsius, while current policies point to a rise of about 2.6 degrees Celsius by 2100.

#### An alternative emerges to check Japan's bear troubles

**In short:** As rising bear attacks bring fear across Japan — six people killed nationwide since April 1 after a record 13 fatal attacks last fiscal year, and a record 14,000 bears culled last fiscal year — Nagano's pioneering coexistence programme since 1995 offers an alternative to mass culling through trapping, tagging, and releasing bears, Karelian Bear Dogs, and more than 3,000 km of fencing. Scientist Akiko Takii argues the approach has helped form a healthy bear population and limit dangerous encounters, with annual sightings broadly stable at around 1,400 in recent years, compared with 59 encounters and four deaths in Akita and five fatalities in Iwate last year.

Scientist Akiko Takii argues the approach has helped form a healthy bear population and limit dangerous encounters, with annual sightings broadly stable at around 1,400 in recent years, compared with 59 encounters and four deaths in Akita and five fatalities in Iwate last year.

#### Tilak guides South Zone to Duleep Trophy final

**In short:** Skipper Tilak Varma played a second successive influential knock, guiding South Zone to the Duleep Trophy final with a seven-wicket win over North Zone on the fourth and final day of the semifinal in Bengaluru on Wednesday. Chasing 181, South Zone finished 182 for three as Tilak remained unbeaten on 51 and Ravichandran Smaran was 32 not out in an unbroken 80-run fourth-wicket stand, and South will face East Zone in the title clash in Chennai from September 6 after Tilak's unbeaten 116 in the first innings had helped secure a 117-run lead.

#### Asia Cup: India must fix batting frailties against Hong Kong

**In short:** India will need to sort out batting frailties for a more complete performance when they face Hong Kong in their second match of the Women's T20 Asia Cup in Dubai on Thursday. After sitting at 101 for two after 10 overs in their opening win over Thailand, India lost seven wickets for 29 runs to finish at 159 for nine before winning by 94 runs; Shafali Verma scored 64 and Deepti Sharma took three wickets without conceding a run, but Smriti Mandhana, Pratika Rawal and skipper Harmanpreet Kaur will want better returns ahead of bigger battles including against Pakistan and the Asian Games in Japan.

#### Gambhir, Agarkar to review injury list, World Cup plans

**In short:** Head coach Gautam Gambhir and selection committee chairman Ajit Agarkar will meet BCCI officials in New Delhi on Thursday to discuss injury management for key players ahead of the October–November New Zealand tour and the roadmap for next year's ODI World Cup. The June–July UK debacle is expected to be reviewed, but stakeholders including secretary Devajit Saikia want to look ahead, with COE head VVS Laxman also expected to attend; Agarkar and Gambhir are seeking clarity on centrally contracted injured players such as Jasprit Bumrah and Hardik Pandya at the Bengaluru COE, and on fitness timelines for Bumrah, Washington Sundar, Nitish Kumar Reddy and Harshit Rana named subject to fitness for the Afghanistan T20I series from September 13.

#### US Open: Zverev outlasts Sonego in wild five-setter to reach second round

**In short:** Top-seeded Alexander Zverev scraped into the US Open second round on Tuesday, beating 89th-ranked Lorenzo Sonego 6-4, 3-6, 6-7 (7/9), 7-5, 6-4 on Arthur Ashe Stadium after four hours and 53 minutes, finishing at 1:55 a.m. Zverev said it was nearly five hours for a first-round match and the longest first-round match he had ever played, notching his seventh win in seven meetings against Sonego and booking a second-round meeting with France's Quentin Halys while avoiding becoming the first men's top seed in 23 years to fall in a Grand Slam first round.

Top-seeded Alexander Zverev scraped into the US Open second round on Tuesday, beating 89th-ranked Lorenzo Sonego 6-4, 3-6, 6-7 (7/9), 7-5, 6-4 on Arthur Ashe Stadium after four hours and 53 minutes, finishing at 1:55 a.m. Zverev said it was nearly five hours for a first-round match and the longest first-round match he had ever played, notching his seventh win in seven meetings against Sonego and booking a second-round meeting with France's Quentin Halys while avoiding becoming the first men's top seed in 23 years to fall in a Grand Slam first round.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** US Open: Zverev outlasts Sonego in wild five-setter to reach second round
- **Told:** Top-seeded Alexander Zverev scraped into the US Open second round on Tuesday, beating 89th-ranked Lorenzo Sonego 6-4, 3-6, 6-7 (7/9), 7-5, 6-4 on Arthur Ashe Stadium after four hours and 53 minutes, finishing at 1:55 a.m.
- **Purpose:** Zverev said it was nearly five hours for a first-round match and the longest first-round match he had ever played, notching his seventh win in seven meetings against Sonego and booking a second-round meeting with France's Quentin Halys while avoiding becoming the first men's top seed in 23 years to fall in a Grand Slam first round.

**Important points**

#### Gill, Bumrah slip; Padikkal moves up in Test rankings

**In short:** Captain Shubman Gill slipped two positions to ninth among batters and Jasprit Bumrah dropped one spot among bowlers in the latest ICC Test rankings released on Wednesday, while Devdutt Padikkal rose five places to 54th among Indians. Gill returned with only one half-century across three innings on the Sri Lanka tour, wicketkeeper-batter Rishabh Pant remains the highest-ranked Indian batter at seventh, Yashasvi Jaiswal is unchanged at 11th, and Padikkal moved up after scoring 328 runs across two Tests with two centuries on the tour.

#### Selector Auqib Javed wants inquiry into Imam's injury, defends recalling 7 players

**In short:** Pakistan Cricket Board selection committee member Aaqib Javed has questioned the severity of Imam-ul-Haq's calf injury and called for an inquiry into treatment protocols after the opener did not bat during Pakistan's 194-run loss to England in the second Test at Lord's last Sunday. Javed told Geo TV that an inquiry will be conducted, asking how Imam failed to come out to bat in both innings after pulling a muscle, and said action will be taken while defending the panel's decision to recall seven players.

#### 'Playing Brazil, Uruguay will be turning point for Indian football'

**In short:** India head coach Khalid Jamil said playing five-time world champions Brazil and two-time winners Uruguay at home is a dream come true and will prove a turning point in the history of Indian football while helping prepare for next year's Asia Cup qualifiers. Jamil told PTI that India is playing Brazil for the first time in history, the main target is 2027 Asia Cup qualification, and that with full fan support the players will be motivated to perform well against top teams that will help them gain confidence through more matches.

#### From Tiptur's plantations to kitchens, industries and agriculture reimagined

**In short:** India produces more than 31% of the world's coconuts, with Karnataka contributing around 28%, and Tiptur in Tumakuru — known as Kalpataru Naadu — is bidding for a GI tag for its Tiptur Tall variety prized for copra; the feature traces coconut's journey from tender nuts and copra to coir, cocopeat, virgin oil, neera products, charcoal, timber and leaf crafts while noting pest, disease and groundwater challenges facing farmers.

#### Classrooms and its many avatars

**In short:** Government teachers across Karnataka are reimagining classrooms beyond textbooks: Naveen Kumar R U teaches science through music and drama in Tiptur, Hanumesh T Kattimani takes Raichur students to historical sites like Maski, Pallavi Varma Patil runs a school vegetable garden in Devanahalli, and Asha Potadar of Belagavi spends her salary to keep a neighbouring school's LKG-UKG classes running after enrolment rose from 23 to 53.

#### The endurance blueprint: Mastering hydration for workouts and long runs

**In short:** Hydration is often called the “fourth macronutrient” of athletic endurance, and fluid management directly dictates endurance capacity, cardiovascular output, and recovery speed—losing as little as 2% of body weight in fluids impairs aerobic performance, elevates heart rate, and increases perceived exertion, while thirst is a delayed indicator of loss. The piece explains how dehydration affects cardiovascular efficiency, thermoregulation, and nutrient delivery; warning signs from mild dehydration (1–3% body weight loss) through heat exhaustion and heat stroke; how to calculate personal sweat rate via a sweat test; and a three-phase strategy covering pre-hydration (24 hours to 30 minutes before), during-workout replacement every 15–20 minutes (16–28 oz per hour), and post-run recovery at 125–150% of weight lost over 2–4 hours.

Hydration is often called the “fourth macronutrient” of athletic endurance, and fluid management directly dictates endurance capacity, cardiovascular output, and recovery speed—losing as little as 2% of body weight in fluids impairs aerobic performance, elevates heart rate, and increases perceived exertion, while thirst is a delayed indicator of loss. The piece explains how dehydration affects cardiovascular efficiency, thermoregulation, and nutrient delivery; warning signs from mild dehydration (1–3% body weight loss) through heat exhaustion and heat stroke; how to calculate personal sweat rate via a sweat test; and a three-phase strategy covering pre-hydration (24 hours to 30 minutes before), during-workout replacement every 15–20 minutes (16–28 oz per hour), and post-run recovery at 125–150% of weight lost over 2–4 hours. It also contrasts plain water with electrolyte replacement, noting sweat carries roughly 200–1,500 mg of sodium per liter alongside potassium, magnesium, and calcium.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** endurance blueprint: Mastering hydration for workouts and long runs
- **Told:** Hydration is often called the “fourth macronutrient” of athletic endurance, and fluid management directly dictates endurance capacity, cardiovascular output, and recovery speed—losing as little as 2% of body weight in fluids impairs aerobic performance, elevates heart rate, and increases perceived exertion, while thirst is a delayed indicator of loss.
- **Purpose:** It also contrasts plain water with electrolyte replacement, noting sweat carries roughly 200–1,500 mg of sodium per liter alongside potassium, magnesium, and calcium.

**Important points**
- It also contrasts plain water with electrolyte replacement, noting sweat carries roughly 200–1,500 mg of sodium per liter alongside potassium, magnesium, and calcium.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### How obesity increases the risk of spine disorders and chronic back pain

**In short:** Back pain has become one of the most common health problems even as obesity has emerged as one of the fastest-growing public health challenges, yet fewer people realize that excess body weight places tremendous stress on the spine—patients in their thirties and forties are increasingly seen with accelerated spinal wear from weight rather than age alone. Every additional kilogram increases mechanical load on vertebrae, intervertebral discs, facet joints, and supporting muscles, accelerating degeneration and contributing to chronic pain; obesity is a modifiable risk factor linked to chronic low back pain, slipped disc (disc herniation), lumbar canal stenosis, facet joint arthritis, and degenerative disc disease.

Back pain has become one of the most common health problems even as obesity has emerged as one of the fastest-growing public health challenges, yet fewer people realize that excess body weight places tremendous stress on the spine—patients in their thirties and forties are increasingly seen with accelerated spinal wear from weight rather than age alone. Every additional kilogram increases mechanical load on vertebrae, intervertebral discs, facet joints, and supporting muscles, accelerating degeneration and contributing to chronic pain; obesity is a modifiable risk factor linked to chronic low back pain, slipped disc (disc herniation), lumbar canal stenosis, facet joint arthritis, and degenerative disc disease. Weight loss can reduce mechanical stress, improve walking ability, decrease inflammation, and improve outcomes after spine surgery, though severely compressed nerves or advanced cord compression may still need medication, injections, or surgery; management also includes physiotherapy, core strengthening, healthy nutrition, and prevention through maintaining a healthy weight throughout adulthood.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** How obesity increases the risk of spine disorders and chronic back pain
- **Told:** Back pain has become one of the most common health problems even as obesity has emerged as one of the fastest-growing public health challenges, yet fewer people realize that excess body weight places tremendous stress on the spine—patients in their thirties and forties are increasingly seen with accelerated spinal wear from weight rather than age alone.
- **Purpose:** Weight loss can reduce mechanical stress, improve walking ability, decrease inflammation, and improve outcomes after spine surgery, though severely compressed nerves or advanced cord compression may still need medication, injections, or surgery; management also includes physiotherapy, core strengthening, healthy nutrition, and prevention through maintaining a healthy weight throughout adulthood.

**Important points**
- Weight loss can reduce mechanical stress, improve walking ability, decrease inflammation, and improve outcomes after spine surgery, though severely compressed nerves or advanced cord compression may still need medication, injections, or surgery.
- management also includes physiotherapy, core strengthening, healthy nutrition, and prevention through maintaining a healthy weight throughout adulthood.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### Brighten your smile naturally with activated charcoal

**In short:** Activated charcoal powder is derived from carbonaceous sources such as coconut husk, peat, wood, coir, nutshells, lignite, coal, and petroleum pitch through chemical or physical activation, yielding a fine, black, tasteless, odorless powder available at medical stores and pharmacies, typically in tablet form. It works as a teeth whitener by binding to rough tooth surfaces where stains, plaques, and yellow discoloration accumulate, using its large pores and adhesive properties to remove plaque, food particles, and surface stains; continuous use can noticeably improve tooth color.

Activated charcoal powder is derived from carbonaceous sources such as coconut husk, peat, wood, coir, nutshells, lignite, coal, and petroleum pitch through chemical or physical activation, yielding a fine, black, tasteless, odorless powder available at medical stores and pharmacies, typically in tablet form. It works as a teeth whitener by binding to rough tooth surfaces where stains, plaques, and yellow discoloration accumulate, using its large pores and adhesive properties to remove plaque, food particles, and surface stains; continuous use can noticeably improve tooth color. Users grind one tablet into powder, mix with a few drops of water to form a paste, brush gently, and rinse thoroughly—though the article notes it is not suitable for decayed or cavity-stricken teeth.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Brighten your smile naturally with activated charcoal
- **Told:** Activated charcoal powder is derived from carbonaceous sources such as coconut husk, peat, wood, coir, nutshells, lignite, coal, and petroleum pitch through chemical or physical activation, yielding a fine, black, tasteless, odorless powder available at medical stores and pharmacies, typically in tablet form.

#### Moving towards a universal vaccine against pneumonia and meningitis

**In short:** British scientists report an early step towards a universal pneumococcal vaccine using reverse vaccinology: proteins zinc metalloprotease B, pneumococcal adherence virulence factor A, and YfhO-like, mixed with immune boosters CpG and chitosan in a formulation called ZPY-CpG-Ch. In laboratory mice exposed to lethal doses of hypervirulent S.

British scientists report an early step towards a universal pneumococcal vaccine using reverse vaccinology: proteins zinc metalloprotease B, pneumococcal adherence virulence factor A, and YfhO-like, mixed with immune boosters CpG and chitosan in a formulation called ZPY-CpG-Ch. In laboratory mice exposed to lethal doses of hypervirulent S. pneumoniae serotype 1, vaccinated animals showed 80–100% survival, comparable to PCV13; the vaccine also fully protected mice against non-vaccine serotypes 11A and 33F and offered 50% protection against serotype 8, though results remain preliminary and tested against only four serotypes.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Moving towards a universal vaccine against pneumonia and meningitis
- **Told:** British scientists report an early step towards a universal pneumococcal vaccine using reverse vaccinology: proteins zinc metalloprotease B, pneumococcal adherence virulence factor A, and YfhO-like, mixed with immune boosters CpG and chitosan in a formulation called ZPY-CpG-Ch.
- **Purpose:** pneumoniae serotype 1, vaccinated animals showed 80–100% survival, comparable to PCV13; the vaccine also fully protected mice against non-vaccine serotypes 11A and 33F and offered 50% protection against serotype 8, though results remain preliminary and tested against only four serotypes.

**Important points**
- pneumoniae serotype 1, vaccinated animals showed 80–100% survival, comparable to PCV13.
- the vaccine also fully protected mice against non-vaccine serotypes 11A and 33F and offered 50% protection against serotype 8, though results remain preliminary and tested against only four serotypes.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.16_

#### Know your English

**In short:** S. Upendran explains that cousins are the children of one's aunt or uncle while a half brother shares only one parent; that 'on the qui vive' means alert and watchful; that an escort is a person given for respect or protection whereas a convoy refers to accompanying vehicles rather than people; and that a Molotov cocktail is a homemade firebomb of inflammable liquid in a bottle with a lit wick, named after Soviet Foreign Minister V.M.

#### A colonial gaze: how the British imagined Bastar and its people

**In short:** An excerpt from Shantanu Nandan Sharma's Rebel Bastar describes how colonial accounts portrayed Bastar as a primitive woodland of cannibals — including Henry Harpur Spry's claim that its people 'sacrifice and eat their fellow creatures' without ever visiting — while flawed censuses produced wildly unreliable figures: the 1866 headcount recorded 2,69,684 people but the 1872 census showed an inexplicable 70% drop to 78,856, with J.W. Neill noting children were not enumerated in many villages and enumerators recorded colourful but inaccurate professions such as 'con man', 'pimp', and 'idiot'.

### Other

#### China's laser mosquito killer

**In short:** A Chinese-developed device that uses lasers to detect and eliminate mosquitoes has moved into mass production, with the first units set to reach buyers before the end of August, developer Photon Matrix Lab said in an update on crowdfunding platform Indiegogo. The portable unit from Changzhou-based Photon Matrix Lab combines LiDAR, millimeter-wave radar and AI-powered computer vision to spot mosquitoes and similarly sized flying insects, then fires a precision laser to eliminate them mid-air; production began this month and an official online store is selling infrared and blue-laser versions directly to consumers.

A Chinese-developed device that uses lasers to detect and eliminate mosquitoes has moved into mass production, with the first units set to reach buyers before the end of August, developer Photon Matrix Lab said in an update on crowdfunding platform Indiegogo. The portable unit from Changzhou-based Photon Matrix Lab combines LiDAR, millimeter-wave radar and AI-powered computer vision to spot mosquitoes and similarly sized flying insects, then fires a precision laser to eliminate them mid-air; production began this month and an official online store is selling infrared and blue-laser versions directly to consumers. Similar systems exist outside China — Israeli company Bzigo's Iris ($299) locates mosquitoes with computer vision and infrared sensors but leaves elimination to the user — while Photon Matrix's claimed performance figures have not yet been independently verified and broader adoption will likely hinge on proven effectiveness, safety, reliability and price.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** China's laser mosquito killer
- **Told:** A Chinese-developed device that uses lasers to detect and eliminate mosquitoes has moved into mass production, with the first units set to reach buyers before the end of August, developer Photon Matrix Lab said in an update on crowdfunding platform Indiegogo.
- **Purpose:** Similar systems exist outside China — Israeli company Bzigo's Iris ($299) locates mosquitoes with computer vision and infrared sensors but leaves elimination to the user — while Photon Matrix's claimed performance figures have not yet been independently verified and broader adoption will likely hinge on proven effectiveness, safety, reliability and price.

**Important points**
- Similar systems exist outside China — Israeli company Bzigo's Iris ($299) locates mosquitoes with computer vision and infrared sensors but leaves elimination to the user — while Photon Matrix's claimed performance figures have not yet been independently verified and broader adoption will likely hinge on proven effectiveness, safety, reliability and price.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### 'Discipline' trade-off

**In short:** Zoho founder Sridhar Vembu argues on X that leaning too hard on "discipline" can quietly kill the initiative organisations need, contrasting firms where employees look upward for direction against those where they act on their own judgment and push back on each other. He says organisations heavy on metrics tend toward the first model — predictable but only as good as leadership's calls — while initiative-led teams feel messier, argue more, and often rely on the market rather than internal process to impose order; attempting both a "disciplined culture of seizing the initiative" tends to backfire.

Zoho founder Sridhar Vembu argues on X that leaning too hard on "discipline" can quietly kill the initiative organisations need, contrasting firms where employees look upward for direction against those where they act on their own judgment and push back on each other. He says organisations heavy on metrics tend toward the first model — predictable but only as good as leadership's calls — while initiative-led teams feel messier, argue more, and often rely on the market rather than internal process to impose order; attempting both a "disciplined culture of seizing the initiative" tends to backfire. The post comes as Zoho contends with memory prices up roughly 500% over the past year, partly fuelled by AI data-centre demand, alongside rising AI token costs; Vembu says the firm has so far avoided passing costs to customers but maintaining that is growing tougher, and argues software development must shift toward leaner code as the assumption of cheap memory no longer holds.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 'Discipline' trade-off
- **Told:** Zoho founder Sridhar Vembu argues on X that leaning too hard on "discipline" can quietly kill the initiative organisations need, contrasting firms where employees look upward for direction against those where they act on their own judgment and push back on each other.
- **Purpose:** The post comes as Zoho contends with memory prices up roughly 500% over the past year, partly fuelled by AI data-centre demand, alongside rising AI token costs; Vembu says the firm has so far avoided passing costs to customers but maintaining that is growing tougher, and argues software development must shift toward leaner code as the assumption of cheap memory no longer holds.

**Important points**
- The post comes as Zoho contends with memory prices up roughly 500% over the past year, partly fuelled by AI data-centre demand, alongside rising AI token costs.
- Vembu says the firm has so far avoided passing costs to customers but maintaining that is growing tougher, and argues software development must shift toward leaner code as the assumption of cheap memory no longer holds.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### It works — but we do not know how

**In short:** The Science Quiz column poses five questions on drugs and therapies whose mechanisms remain incompletely understood: the mood stabiliser lithium; general anaesthetics that induce unconsciousness, amnesia, and immobility during surgery; high-temperature superconductors in YBCO compounds; metformin (Glucophage) for type II diabetes; and ketamine, derived from phencyclidine, which at sub-anesthetic doses can lift severe depression within hours. Answers to the August 27 quiz on oil drilling are also published.

## Technology

### Top 5 — Technology

#### 1. SC quashes Rs 425-cr penalty over AI-generated precedents

**In short:** The Supreme Court on Wednesday set aside a Rs 425.27-crore customs penalty imposed on diamond trader Vijay Ghanshyam Gadiya after finding that the adjudicating authority had relied on non-existent or incorrectly cited judicial precedents that appeared to have been generated or hallucinated by artificial intelligence. A bench of Justices Dipankar Datta and Sheel Nagu allowed the civil appeal and quashed both the Gujarat High Court order and the original customs adjudication order, which had been imposed by the Additional Commissioner of Customs, Surat, on the allegation that Gadiya had mis-declared a consignment of natural diamonds as lab-grown diamonds.

The Supreme Court on Wednesday set aside a Rs 425.27-crore customs penalty imposed on diamond trader Vijay Ghanshyam Gadiya after finding that the adjudicating authority had relied on non-existent or incorrectly cited judicial precedents that appeared to have been generated or hallucinated by artificial intelligence. A bench of Justices Dipankar Datta and Sheel Nagu allowed the civil appeal and quashed both the Gujarat High Court order and the original customs adjudication order, which had been imposed by the Additional Commissioner of Customs, Surat, on the allegation that Gadiya had mis-declared a consignment of natural diamonds as lab-grown diamonds. The court said decisions relying on fake or hallucinated material as precedent are invalid and that courts must adopt a zero-tolerance policy toward producing, citing, or using AI-generated precedents without verification.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** SC quashes Rs 425-cr penalty over AI-generated precedents
- **Told:** The Supreme Court on Wednesday set aside a Rs 425.27-crore customs penalty imposed on diamond trader Vijay Ghanshyam Gadiya after finding that the adjudicating authority had relied on non-existent or incorrectly cited judicial precedents that appeared to have been generated or hallucinated by artificial intelligence.
- **Purpose:** The court said decisions relying on fake or hallucinated material as precedent are invalid and that courts must adopt a zero-tolerance policy toward producing, citing, or using AI-generated precedents without verification.

**Important points**
- The court said decisions relying on fake or hallucinated material as precedent are invalid and that courts must adopt a zero-tolerance policy toward producing, citing, or using AI-generated precedents without verification.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1; Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.5_

#### 2. Juvenile held for creating fake Facebook account in IPS officer's name, duping people

**In short:** A juvenile from Rajasthan was apprehended for allegedly creating a fake Facebook account in the name of IPS officer Narayana M, Deputy Commissioner of Police (Electronic City Division) in Bengaluru, and collecting money from people on various pretexts; police recovered ₹2 lakh in cash allegedly obtained through fake bank accounts. A case was registered at Hebbagodi police station after the accused used the officer's photograph to send friend requests and demand money; investigation busted an organised network operating from Kota Khurd village in Rajasthan, with the juvenile revealing associates had created fake accounts in the names of several IAS and IPS officers across the country.

A juvenile from Rajasthan was apprehended for allegedly creating a fake Facebook account in the name of IPS officer Narayana M, Deputy Commissioner of Police (Electronic City Division) in Bengaluru, and collecting money from people on various pretexts; police recovered ₹2 lakh in cash allegedly obtained through fake bank accounts. A case was registered at Hebbagodi police station after the accused used the officer's photograph to send friend requests and demand money; investigation busted an organised network operating from Kota Khurd village in Rajasthan, with the juvenile revealing associates had created fake accounts in the names of several IAS and IPS officers across the country. On August 25 the juvenile was sent to a rehabilitation centre in Devanahalli, and police advised the public to lodge complaints if approached for money through such fake accounts.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Juvenile held for creating fake Facebook account in IPS officer's name, duping people
- **Told:** A juvenile from Rajasthan was apprehended for allegedly creating a fake Facebook account in the name of IPS officer Narayana M, Deputy Commissioner of Police (Electronic City Division) in Bengaluru, and collecting money from people on various pretexts; police recovered ₹2 lakh in cash allegedly obtained through fake bank accounts.
- **Purpose:** On August 25 the juvenile was sent to a rehabilitation centre in Devanahalli, and police advised the public to lodge complaints if approached for money through such fake accounts.

_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.2_

#### 3. Would AI disaster force humanity to act?

**In short:** Experts in Silicon Valley think that within the next couple of years there will be an extraordinary takeoff for artificial intelligence, described as "recursive self-improvement" — the point at which AI itself trains each successive model, resulting in exponential development of something that in many significant respects is more intelligent than humans. Elon Musk predicts "an age of amazing abundance" but also sees a 10–20% chance of killer robots murdering us all, while Geoffrey Hinton, one of the intellectual founding fathers of AI, told an interviewer in 2023: "My intuition is, we're toast." As Robert Wright puts it in his book The God Test, the near-term future holds a wide array of not-implausible paths for humankind that would be wildly transformative — but whether that means heaven or hell remains sharply contested.

#### 4. Hyundai India MD seeks long-term policy

**In short:** Hyundai Motor India MD and CEO Tarun Garg on Wednesday called for a consistent long-term policy framework to support scale and accelerate technology and component development, while urging the country's auto industry to move beyond exporting conventional components to advanced technologies, engineering capabilities and innovation. Addressing the 66th Annual Session of the Automotive Component Manufacturers Association of India (ACMA), Garg said India needs to accelerate efforts to build domestic capabilities in critical technologies such as semiconductor chips, EV battery cells and power electronics.

Hyundai Motor India MD and CEO Tarun Garg on Wednesday called for a consistent long-term policy framework to support scale and accelerate technology and component development, while urging the country's auto industry to move beyond exporting conventional components to advanced technologies, engineering capabilities and innovation. Addressing the 66th Annual Session of the Automotive Component Manufacturers Association of India (ACMA), Garg said India needs to accelerate efforts to build domestic capabilities in critical technologies such as semiconductor chips, EV battery cells and power electronics. He said the government on its part needs to provide consistent long-term policy that supports scale and accelerates the development of technologies and components.

**What this is**

#### 5. Rising sea levels, millions at risk, but are we ready?

**In short:** A UN report warns millions in cities such as Kolkata and Mumbai face immediate risk of losing homes to rising sea levels and permanent inundation, with NASA and IPCC projections putting roughly 12 Indian coastal cities and towns at risk of submersion by up to 2.7 to 3 feet; Chennai, Kochi, Visakhapatnam and Mangaluru are among those facing accelerated rise, cyclone intensity or imminent danger. By 2040, more than 10% of land in Mumbai, Yanam and Thoothukudi could submerge, while India’s adaptation efforts include ₹1,000 crore for resettling displaced coastal populations and ₹1,500 crore for erosion mitigation under the 15th Finance Commission, though the article notes policy remains diffused and insufficiently integrated on the ground.

- **Purpose:** By 2040, more than 10% of land in Mumbai, Yanam and Thoothukudi could submerge, while India’s adaptation efforts include ₹1,000 crore for resettling displaced coastal populations and ₹1,500 crore for erosion mitigation under the 15th Finance Commission, though the article notes policy remains diffused and insufficiently integrated on the ground.

**Important points**
- A UN report warns millions in cities such as Kolkata and Mumbai face immediate risk of losing homes to rising sea levels and permanent inundation, with NASA and IPCC projections putting roughly 12 Indian coastal cities and towns at risk of submersion by up to 2.7 to 3 feet; Chennai, Kochi, Visakhapatnam and Mangaluru are among those facing accelerated rise, cyclone intensity or imminent danger.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.6_

### More technology

#### Should AI be taxed?

**In short:** Warnings that AI will take jobs have intensified as thousands of employees worldwide receive layoff emails on a near-monthly basis — what Indian economist Raghuram Rajan has called a "jobocalypse." Rajan, former Governor of the Reserve Bank of India and a member of the US Federal Reserve policy review panel, has suggested levying a tax on corporate firms' use of artificial intelligence to help governments continue earning tax revenues that nations would have received from a human workforce. In an August 2026 column titled "How Corporations Can Mitigate an AI Jobocalypse," he wrote that AI-related job displacement is coming, though no one knows how fast it will proceed, how far it will go, or which sectors it will affect most.
