#### AI eats traffic, websites block AI, making reliable information scarce

**In short:** For 30 years the world wide web has run on a social contract in which most sites are free for search engines to access but users who draw on their content give credit by linking to the source — a contract now collapsing as AI tools crawl sites not to link to them but to train models and generate answers. Cloudflare, which manages 30% or more of the top 10,000 internet sites, estimates over half of all web traffic is now AI bots; as websites lose traffic and revenue, many are blocking AI scrapers, meaning AI results depend more on low-quality websites, including many generated by AI.

For 30 years the world wide web has run on a social contract in which most sites are free for search engines to access but users who draw on their content give credit by linking to the source — a contract now collapsing as AI tools crawl sites not to link to them but to train models and generate answers. Cloudflare, which manages 30% or more of the top 10,000 internet sites, estimates over half of all web traffic is now AI bots; as websites lose traffic and revenue, many are blocking AI scrapers, meaning AI results depend more on low-quality websites, including many generated by AI. From September 15, Cloudflare sites will block AI crawlers by default on pages containing advertising — potentially removing up to 30% of the world's top sites from Google AI Overviews summaries — while studies already show around one in six sources used by AI search tools is itself an AI-generated website, raising the risk of model collapse as AI models train on more AI text.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** AI eats traffic, websites block AI, making reliable information scarce
- **Told:** For 30 years the world wide web has run on a social contract in which most sites are free for search engines to access but users who draw on their content give credit by linking to the source — a contract now collapsing as AI tools crawl sites not to link to them but to train models and generate answers.
- **Purpose:** From September 15, Cloudflare sites will block AI crawlers by default on pages containing advertising — potentially removing up to 30% of the world's top sites from Google AI Overviews summaries — while studies already show around one in six sources used by AI search tools is itself an AI-generated website, raising the risk of model collapse as AI models train on more AI text.

**Important points**
- From September 15.
- Cloudflare sites will block AI crawlers by default on pages containing advertising — potentially removing up to 30% of the world's top sites from Google AI Overviews summaries — while studies already show around one in six sources used by AI search tools is itself an AI-generated website, raising the risk of model collapse as AI models train on more AI text.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### ChatGPT drops custom stickers for mobile users

**In short:** OpenAI has rolled out a feature called Stickers in ChatGPT, allowing users to generate personalised sticker packs directly from text prompts and share them in everyday conversations; it went live globally for mobile ChatGPT users, according to an OpenAI announcement. Users can remix stickers with emojis and pick from as many as 18 visual styles — including Holographic, Anime and 3D — and the feature is free and available to all mobile ChatGPT users worldwide, though OpenAI has not specified whether it will extend to desktop or web versions.

Users can remix stickers with emojis and pick from as many as 18 visual styles — including Holographic, Anime and 3D — and the feature is free and available to all mobile ChatGPT users worldwide, though OpenAI has not specified whether it will extend to desktop or web versions. Code strings referencing a ChatGPT Stickers tool and a native WhatsApp export option were spotted in the Android app in early August, ahead of today's formal rollout.

## Opinion & Explainers

### Migration and Kerala's choices (Opinion)

**In short:** Kerala policymakers seeking to reverse emigration must ask whether the state's labour and academic markets can absorb migrants, argue Sonal Kuruvilla and Fahad Hasin, noting that people leave when second-best local options pay too little and that building thick urban labour markets with minimal regulatory friction is the real policy challenge rather than stopping departures.

- Kerala policymakers seeking to reverse emigration must ask whether the state's labour and academic markets can absorb migrants, argue Sonal Kuruvilla and Fahad Hasin, noting that people leave when second-best local options pay too little and that building thick urban labour markets with minimal regulatory friction is the real policy challenge rather than stopping departures.

### Many layers (Opinion)

**In short:** Since the 1960s India has balanced affordable food prices with remunerative farmer prices. The government banned onion exports from December 2023 to May 2024, imposed a $550/tonne minimum export price and a 40% export duty—reduced to 20% in September 2024 and abolished in April 2025.

- Since the 1960s India has balanced affordable food prices with remunerative farmer prices.
- The government banned onion exports from December 2023 to May 2024, imposed a $550/tonne minimum export price and a 40% export duty—reduced to 20% in September 2024 and abolished in April 2025.
- During a bounteous rabi harvest, onion farmers in Maharashtra argued the Centre's procurement price of ₹12.35/kg would not cover cultivation costs.
- it was raised to up to ₹26.45/kg, but many farmers could not capture that value, including some who sold at ₹1/kg.
- Abnormal rainfall at harvest and a 5%–7% drop in the kharif crop in Maharashtra followed.
- Tamil Nadu targeted a subsidy to buy 1,000 tonnes of onions and distribute 1 kg per ration card at ₹35.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.6_

### 'Early Harvest' — larger but not necessarily safer (Opinion)

**In short:** At August 25, 2026 special representative talks in Beijing, an August 26 "Eight Points" document restored "Early and Substantial Harvest" for boundary delimitation and border management—India's coinage from around 2019 as a counter to China's Sikkim-only proposal. A genuine Early Harvest requires highest-watershed delineation, trijunction points excluded per the 2012 Common Understanding, and terms of reference extending India's 2019 proposal.

- At August 25, 2026 special representative talks in Beijing, an August 26 "Eight Points" document restored "Early and Substantial Harvest" for boundary delimitation and border management—India's coinage from around 2019 as a counter to China's Sikkim-only proposal.
- A genuine Early Harvest requires highest-watershed delineation, trijunction points excluded per the 2012 Common Understanding, and terms of reference extending India's 2019 proposal.
- The Eight Points are silent on the Medog County project on the Yarlung Tsangpo.
- a September meeting should convey India's concerns about the world's largest hydropower plant near the border in an earthquake-prone zone.

### Unconditional dependence (Opinion)

**In short:** Swami Sivayoganandhaji writes that bhakti yoga suits the Kali Yuga, citing Lord Krishna's teaching that a devotee whose mind is fixed on Him every moment with complete faith and unconditional dependence is the best exponent of yoga. He distinguishes three worshippers — self-focused, those who treat themselves and God as equally important, and those who leave everything to God like the gopikas of Brindavan — and urges disciplining the wandering mind, as Bhishma did among the top bhakti acharyas.

- Swami Sivayoganandhaji writes that bhakti yoga suits the Kali Yuga, citing Lord Krishna's teaching that a devotee whose mind is fixed on Him every moment with complete faith and unconditional dependence is the best exponent of yoga.
- He distinguishes three worshippers — self-focused, those who treat themselves and God as equally important, and those who leave everything to God like the gopikas of Brindavan — and urges disciplining the wandering mind, as Bhishma did among the top bhakti acharyas.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.10_

---

### Constitutional faultlines in FCRA Bill (Opinion)

**In short:** Thajaswini C.B. and Siddharth Dev Prasad argue the Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Amendment Bill, 2026 goes beyond regulating foreign contributions by creating a statutory 'Designated Authority' to oversee the vesting, supervision, management, and disposal of foreign contributions and assets when an organisation's FCRA certificate is cancelled, surrendered, or ceases to exist — potentially extending to management of the organisation's activities — raising constitutional questions about executive overreach, proportionality, and whether safeguards for restoration, revision, and appeal are sufficiently clear and timely.

- Thajaswini C.B.
- and Siddharth Dev Prasad argue the Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Amendment Bill, 2026 goes beyond regulating foreign contributions by creating a statutory 'Designated Authority' to oversee the vesting, supervision, management, and disposal of foreign contributions and assets when an organisation's FCRA certificate is cancelled, surrendered, or ceases to exist — potentially extending to management of the organisation's activities — raising constitutional questions about executive overreach, proportionality, and whether safeguards for restoration, revision, and appeal are sufficiently clear and timely.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.17_
